Any port which is\n64: \t// listening on the default \"0.0.0.0\" address inside a container will be\n65: \t// accessible from the network.\n66: \t// Modifying this array with strategic merge patch may corrupt the data.\n67: \t// For more information See https://github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes/issues/108255.\n68: \t// Cannot be updated.\n69: \tPorts []ContainerPortApplyConfiguration `json:\"ports,omitempty\"`\n70: \t// List of sources to populate environment variables in the container.\n71: \t// The keys defined within a source may consist of any printable ASCII characters except '='.\n72: \t// When a key exists in multiple\n73: \t// sources, the value associated with the last source will take precedence.\n74: \t// Values defined by an Env with a duplicate key will take precedence.\n75: \t// Cannot be updated.\n76: \tEnvFrom []EnvFromSourceApplyConfiguration `json:\"envFrom,omitempty\"`\n77: \t// List of environment variables to set in the container.\n78: \t// Cannot be updated.\n79: \tEnv []EnvVarApplyConfiguration `json:\"env,omitempty\"`\n80: \t// Compute Resources required by this container.\n81: \t// Cannot be updated.\n82: \t// More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/\n83: \tResources *ResourceRequirementsApplyConfiguration `json:\"resources,omitempty\"`\n84: \t// Resources resize policy for the container.\n85: \t// This field cannot be set on ephemeral containers.\n86: \tResizePolicy []ContainerResizePolicyApplyConfiguration `json:\"resizePolicy,omitempty\"`\n87: \t// RestartPolicy defines the restart behavior of individual containers in a pod.\n88: \t// This overrides the pod-level restart policy. When this field is not specified,\n89: \t// the restart behavior is defined by the Pod's restart policy and the container type.\n90: \t// Additionally, setting the RestartPolicy as \"Always\" for the init container will\n91: \t// have the following effect:\n92: \t// this init container will be continually restarted on\n93: \t// exit until all regular containers have terminated. Once all regular\n94: \t// containers have completed, all init containers with restartPolicy \"Always\"\n95: \t// will be shut down. This lifecycle differs from normal init containers and\n96: \t// is often referred to as a \"sidecar\" container. Although this init\n97: \t// container still starts in the init container sequence, it does not wait\n98: \t// for the container to complete before proceeding to the next init\n99: \t// container. Instead, the next init container starts immediately after this\n100: \t// init container is started, or after any startupProbe has successfully\n101: \t// completed.\n102: \tRestartPolicy *corev1.ContainerRestartPolicy `json:\"restartPolicy,omitempty\"`\n103: \t// Represents a list of rules to be checked to determine if the\n104: \t// container should be restarted on exit. The rules are evaluated in\n105: \t// order. Once a rule matches a container exit condition, the remaining\n106: \t// rules are ignored. If no rule matches the container exit condition,\n107: \t// the Container-level restart policy determines the whether the container\n108: \t// is restarted or not. Constraints on the rules:\n109: \t// - At most 20 rules are allowed.\n110: \t// - Rules can have the same action.\n111: \t// - Identical rules are not forbidden in validations.\n112: \t// When rules are specified, container MUST set RestartPolicy explicitly\n113: \t// even it if matches the Pod's RestartPolicy.\n114: \tRestartPolicyRules []ContainerRestartRuleApplyConfiguration `json:\"restartPolicyRules,omitempty\"`\n115: \t// Pod volumes to mount into the container's filesystem.\n116: \t// Cannot be updated.\n117: \tVolumeMounts []VolumeMountApplyConfiguration `json:\"volumeMounts,omitempty\"`\n118: \t// volumeDevices is the list of block devices to be used by the container.\n119: \tVolumeDevices []VolumeDeviceApplyConfiguration `json:\"volumeDevices,omitempty\"`\n120: \t// Periodic probe of container liveness.\n121: \t// Container will be restarted if the probe fails.\n122: \t// Cannot be updated.\n123: \t// More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle#container-probes\n124: \tLivenessProbe *ProbeApplyConfiguration `json:\"livenessProbe,omitempty\"`\n125: \t// Periodic probe of container service readiness.\n126: \t// Container will be removed from service endpoints if the probe fails.\n127: \t// Cannot be updated.\n128: \t// More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/pod-lifecycle#container-probes\n129: \tReadinessProbe *ProbeApplyConfiguration `json:\"readinessProbe,omitempty\"`\n130: \t// StartupProbe indicates that the Pod has successfully initialized.\n131: \t// If specified, no other probes are executed until this completes successfully.\n132: \t// If this probe fails, the Pod will be restarted, just as if the livenessProbe failed.\n133: \t// This can be used to provide different probe parameters at the beginning of a Pod's lifecycle,\n134: \t// when it might take a long time to load data or warm a updated.\n140: \tLifecycle *LifecycleApplyConfiguration `json:\"lifecycle,omitempty\"`\n141: \t// Optional: Path at which the file to which the container's termination message\n142: \t// will be written is mounted into the container's filesystem.\n143: \t// Message written is intended to be brief final status, such as an assertion failure message.\n144: \t// Will be truncated by the node if greater than 4096 bytes. The total message length across\n145: \t// all containers will be limited to 12kb.\n146: \t// Defaults to /dev/termination-log.\n147: \t// Cannot be updated.\n148: \tTerminationMessagePath *string `json:\"terminationMessagePath,omitempty\"`\n149: \t// Indicate how the termination message should be populated. File will use the contents of\n150: \t// terminationMessagePath to populate the container status message on both success and failure.\n151: \t// FallbackToLogsOnError will use the last chunk of container log output if the termination\n152: \t// message file is empty and the container exited with an error.\n153: \t// The log output is limited to 2048 bytes or 80 lines, whichever is smaller.\n154: \t// Defaults to File.\n155: \t// Cannot be updated.\n156: \tTerminationMessagePolicy *corev1.TerminationMessagePolicy `json:\"terminationMessagePolicy,omitempty\"`\n157: \t// Image pull policy.\n158: \t// One of Always, Never, IfNotPresent.\n159: \t// Defaults to Always if :latest tag is specified, or IfNotPresent otherwise.\n160: \t// Cannot be updated.\n161: \t// More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/containers/images#updating-images\n162: \tImagePullPolicy *corev1.PullPolicy `json:\"imagePullPolicy,omitempty\"`\n163: \t// SecurityContext defines the security options the container should be run with.\n164: \t// If set, the fields of SecurityContext override the equivalent fields of PodSecurityContext.\n165: \t// More info: https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/configure-pod-container/security-context/\n166: \tSecurityContext *SecurityContextApplyConfiguration `json:\"securityContext,omitempty\"`\n167: \t// Whether this container should allocate a buffer for stdin in the container runtime. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_01RThovu1ivsoQW4C3dLCQf1","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01D2vxt85qPyzW3W6BVpZXD6","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/staging/src/k8s.io/client-go/applyconfigurations/doc.go","content":"/*\nCopyright The Kubernetes Authors.\n\nLicensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\");\nyou may not use this file except in compliance with the License.\nYou may obtain a copy of the License at\n\n    http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n\nUnless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software\ndistributed under the License is distributed on an \"AS IS\" BASIS,\nWITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.\nSee the License for the specific language governing permissions and\nlimitations under the License.\n*/\n\n/*\nPackage applyconfigurations provides type-safe apply configuration types for\nKubernetes Server-side Apply (SSA).\n\n# Overview\n\nServer-side Apply is a declarative API for creating and updating Kubernetes\nresources. Unlike traditional create/update operations that replace the entire\nresource, Server-side Apply performs a merge of the fields you specify with\nthe existing resource, tracked by field managers.\n\nThis package provides generated apply configuration types that are specifically\ndesigned to work with Server-side Apply. Each apply configuration type\ncorresponds to a Kubernetes API type and is named with an \"ApplyConfiguration\"\nsuffix (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration for Pod).\n\n# The Zero-Value Problem\n\nStandard Go structs cannot be used directly with Server-side Apply due to the\nzero-value problem. In Go, all struct fields have zero values when not\nexplicitly set. For example:\n\n\t// Using a standard Go struct\n\tpod := &corev1.Pod{\n\t\tSpec: corev1.PodSpec{\n\t\t\tActiveDeadlineSeconds: nil, // Is this intentionally unset, or just zero?\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\nWhen encoding this to JSON, there is no way to distinguish between:\n  - A field that was intentionally set to nil (meaning: \"I want to remove this field\")\n  - A field that was never set (meaning: \"I don't care about this field\")\n\nThis distinction is critical for Server-side Apply because it determines field\nownership. If a zero-valued field is included in an apply request, the field\nmanager takes ownership of it, potentially causing conflicts with other field\nmanagers.\n\n# Apply Configuration Types\n\nApply configuration types solve this problem by making all fields pointers or\nslices, allowing the distinction between \"not set\" (nil) and \"explicitly set\":\n\n\t// Using an apply configuration type\n\tpod := corev1.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n\t\tWithSpec(corev1.PodSpec().\n\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1.Container().\n\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n\nIn the example above, only the Name and Image fields of the Container are set.\nOther fields like Command, Args, and WorkingDir remain nil, indicating they\nshould not be included in the apply request.\n\n# With Functions\n\nEach apply configuration type provides builder methods prefixed with \"With\" for\nsetting fields. These methods:\n  - Accept the field value as a parameter\n  - Set the field to that value\n  - Return the receiver for method chaining\n\nFor example:\n\n\tcontainer := corev1.Container().\n\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\").\n\t\tWithPorts(\n\t\t\tcorev1.ContainerPort().\n\t\t\t\tWithContainerPort(80).\n\t\t\t\tWithProtocol(\"TCP\"),\n\t\t)\n\nThis pattern allows you to build complex configurations with a fluent API while\nensuring only explicitly set fields are included in the apply request.\n\n# Using Apply Configurations with Typed Clients\n\nThe typed clients in k8s.io/client-go/kubernetes/typed provide Apply methods\nthat accept apply configurations directly:\n\n\timport (\n\t\t\"context\"\n\t\tmetav1 \"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1\"\n\t\tcorev1ac \"k8s.io/client-go/applyconfigurations/core/v1\"\n\t)\n\n\t// Create an apply configuration\n\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n\n\t// Apply to the cluster\n\tpod, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n\t\tcontext.TODO(),\n\t\tpodApply,\n\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t)\n\nThe FieldManager in ApplyOptions identifies the actor making changes. This is\nrequired for Server-side Apply to track field ownership. If another field\nmanager has already set a field you're trying to modify, you'll receive a\nconflict error unless you use the Force option.\n\n# Controller Support\n\nThis package supports two mechanisms for using Server-side Apply in controllers:\n\nMechanism 1: Recreate Apply Configuration Each Reconciliation\n\nIn this approach, the controller builds the complete apply configuration from\nscratch on each reconciliation:\n\n\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n\t\t// Fetch the source of truth (e.g., a custom resource)\n\t\tmyResource := &myv1.MyResource{}\n\t\tif err := r.Client.Get(ctx, req.NamespacedName, myResource); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Build apply configuration from scratch\n\t\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(myResource.Name, myResource.Namespace).\n\t\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n\t\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n\t\t\t\t\tWithName(\"app\").\n\t\t\t\t\tWithImage(myResource.Spec.Image)))\n\n\t\t// Apply - fields not specified will not be affected\n\t\t_, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(myResource.Namespace).Apply(\n\t\t\tctx,\n\t\t\tpodApply,\n\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t}\n\nThis approach is simple and stateless. Each reconciliation declares the desired\nstate without needing to know the current state.\n\nMechanism 2: Extract/Modify-in-Place/Apply Workflow\n\nFor controllers that need to preserve existing field values while making\ntargeted modifications, the Extract functions provide a way to read back\npreviously applied fields:\n\n\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n\t\t// Fetch the current pod\n\t\tpod, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Get(ctx, req.Name, metav1.GetOptions{})\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Extract the fields this controller previously applied\n\t\tpodApply, err := corev1ac.ExtractPod(pod, \"my-controller\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Modify in place - only changing what we need\n\t\tpodApply.WithLabels(map[string]string{\n\t\t\t\"updated-at\": time.Now().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t\t})\n\n\t\t// Re-apply the modified configuration\n\t\t_, err = r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Apply(\n\t\t\tctx,\n\t\t\tpodApply,\n\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t}\n\nThe Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod, ExtractDeployment) read the managed\nfields from an object and return an apply configuration containing only the\nfields owned by the specified field manager. This allows controllers to:\n  - Preserve fields they previously set\n  - Make targeted modifications without affecting other fields\n  - Avoid accidentally removing fields that were intentionally set\n\nNote that an extracted apply configuration may contain fewer fields than\noriginally applied if another field manager has since taken ownership of some\nfields (via Force option).\n\n# Conflict Resolution\n\nWhen multiple field managers attempt to modify the same field, Server-side Apply\nreturns a conflict error. There are two ways to handle conflicts:\n\n1. Retry with Force: Set Force: true in ApplyOptions to take ownership of\nconflicting fields. Use this when you want to override other managers:\n\n\t_, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n\t\tctx,\n\t\tpodApply,\n\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{\n\t\t\tFieldManager: \"my-controller\",\n\t\t\tForce:        true,\n\t\t},\n\t)\n\n2. Remove the conflicting fields: If your controller doesn't need to own those\nfields, simply don't set them in your apply configuration.\n\n# Package Structure\n\nThe package is organized by API group and version:\n  - applyconfigurations/core/v1 - Core API types (Pod, Service, ConfigMap, etc.)\n  - applyconfigurations/apps/v1 - Apps API types (Deployment, StatefulSet, etc.)\n  - applyconfigurations/batch/v1 - Batch API types (Job, CronJob)\n  - applyconfigurations/meta/v1 - Metadata types (ObjectMeta, TypeMeta, etc.)\n  - And similar subpackages for other API groups\n\nEach subpackage provides:\n  - Apply configuration struct types (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration)\n  - Constructor functions (e.g., Pod(name, namespace string))\n  - Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod(pod *corev1.Pod, fieldManager string))\n  - With builder methods for all fields\n*/\npackage applyconfigurations\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":255,"cache_read_input_tokens":64528,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":255,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"63166e29-212f-4903-8923-2869b605b02f","uuid":"57770f6c-9fb4-4654-9bad-9e0e89bc90bc"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NFiAZUKWtgjTWbJ63qFw74","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→/*\n     2→Copyright The Kubernetes Authors.\n     3→\n     4→Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\");\n     5→you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.\n     6→You may obtain a copy of the License at\n     7→\n     8→    http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n     9→\n    10→Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software\n    11→distributed under the License is distributed on an \"AS IS\" BASIS,\n    12→WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.\n    13→See the License for the specific language governing permissions and\n    14→limitations under the License.\n    15→*/\n    16→\n    17→/*\n    18→Package applyconfigurations provides type-safe apply configuration types for\n    19→Kubernetes Server-side Apply (SSA).\n    20→\n    21→# Overview\n    22→\n    23→Server-side Apply is a declarative API for creating and updating Kubernetes\n    24→resources. Unlike traditional create/update operations that replace the entire\n    25→resource, Server-side Apply performs a merge of the fields you specify with\n    26→the existing resource, tracked by field managers.\n    27→\n    28→This package provides generated apply configuration types that are specifically\n    29→designed to work with Server-side Apply. Each apply configuration type\n    30→corresponds to a Kubernetes API type and is named with an \"ApplyConfiguration\"\n    31→suffix (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration for Pod).\n    32→\n    33→# The Zero-Value Problem\n    34→\n    35→Standard Go structs cannot be used directly with Server-side Apply due to the\n    36→zero-value problem. In Go, all struct fields have zero values when not\n    37→explicitly set. For example:\n    38→\n    39→\t// Using a standard Go struct\n    40→\tpod := &corev1.Pod{\n    41→\t\tSpec: corev1.PodSpec{\n    42→\t\t\tActiveDeadlineSeconds: nil, // Is this intentionally unset, or just zero?\n    43→\t\t},\n    44→\t}\n    45→\n    46→When encoding this to JSON, there is no way to distinguish between:\n    47→  - A field that was intentionally set to nil (meaning: \"I want to remove this field\")\n    48→  - A field that was never set (meaning: \"I don't care about this field\")\n    49→\n    50→This distinction is critical for Server-side Apply because it determines field\n    51→ownership. If a zero-valued field is included in an apply request, the field\n    52→manager takes ownership of it, potentially causing conflicts with other field\n    53→managers.\n    54→\n    55→# Apply Configuration Types\n    56→\n    57→Apply configuration types solve this problem by making all fields pointers or\n    58→slices, allowing the distinction between \"not set\" (nil) and \"explicitly set\":\n    59→\n    60→\t// Using an apply configuration type\n    61→\tpod := corev1.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n    62→\t\tWithSpec(corev1.PodSpec().\n    63→\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1.Container().\n    64→\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n    65→\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n    66→\n    67→In the example above, only the Name and Image fields of the Container are set.\n    68→Other fields like Command, Args, and WorkingDir remain nil, indicating they\n    69→should not be included in the apply request.\n    70→\n    71→# With Functions\n    72→\n    73→Each apply configuration type provides builder methods prefixed with \"With\" for\n    74→setting fields. These methods:\n    75→  - Accept the field value as a parameter\n    76→  - Set the field to that value\n    77→  - Return the receiver for method chaining\n    78→\n    79→For example:\n    80→\n    81→\tcontainer := corev1.Container().\n    82→\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n    83→\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\").\n    84→\t\tWithPorts(\n    85→\t\t\tcorev1.ContainerPort().\n    86→\t\t\t\tWithContainerPort(80).\n    87→\t\t\t\tWithProtocol(\"TCP\"),\n    88→\t\t)\n    89→\n    90→This pattern allows you to build complex configurations with a fluent API while\n    91→ensuring only explicitly set fields are included in the apply request.\n    92→\n    93→# Using Apply Configurations with Typed Clients\n    94→\n    95→The typed clients in k8s.io/client-go/kubernetes/typed provide Apply methods\n    96→that accept apply configurations directly:\n    97→\n    98→\timport (\n    99→\t\t\"context\"\n   100→\t\tmetav1 \"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1\"\n   101→\t\tcorev1ac \"k8s.io/client-go/applyconfigurations/core/v1\"\n   102→\t)\n   103→\n   104→\t// Create an apply configuration\n   105→\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n   106→\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n   107→\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n   108→\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n   109→\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n   110→\n   111→\t// Apply to the cluster\n   112→\tpod, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n   113→\t\tcontext.TODO(),\n   114→\t\tpodApply,\n   115→\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n   116→\t)\n   117→\n   118→The FieldManager in ApplyOptions identifies the actor making changes. This is\n   119→required for Server-side Apply to track field ownership. If another field\n   120→manager has already set a field you're trying to modify, you'll receive a\n   121→conflict error unless you use the Force option.\n   122→\n   123→# Controller Support\n   124→\n   125→This package supports two mechanisms for using Server-side Apply in controllers:\n   126→\n   127→Mechanism 1: Recreate Apply Configuration Each Reconciliation\n   128→\n   129→In this approach, the controller builds the complete apply configuration from\n   130→scratch on each reconciliation:\n   131→\n   132→\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n   133→\t\t// Fetch the source of truth (e.g., a custom resource)\n   134→\t\tmyResource := &myv1.MyResource{}\n   135→\t\tif err := r.Client.Get(ctx, req.NamespacedName, myResource); err != nil {\n   136→\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n   137→\t\t}\n   138→\n   139→\t\t// Build apply configuration from scratch\n   140→\t\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(myResource.Name, myResource.Namespace).\n   141→\t\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n   142→\t\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n   143→\t\t\t\t\tWithName(\"app\").\n   144→\t\t\t\t\tWithImage(myResource.Spec.Image)))\n   145→\n   146→\t\t// Apply - fields not specified will not be affected\n   147→\t\t_, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(myResource.Namespace).Apply(\n   148→\t\t\tctx,\n   149→\t\t\tpodApply,\n   150→\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n   151→\t\t)\n   152→\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n   153→\t}\n   154→\n   155→This approach is simple and stateless. Each reconciliation declares the desired\n   156→state without needing to know the current state.\n   157→\n   158→Mechanism 2: Extract/Modify-in-Place/Apply Workflow\n   159→\n   160→For controllers that need to preserve existing field values while making\n   161→targeted modifications, the Extract functions provide a way to read back\n   162→previously applied fields:\n   163→\n   164→\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n   165→\t\t// Fetch the current pod\n   166→\t\tpod, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Get(ctx, req.Name, metav1.GetOptions{})\n   167→\t\tif err != nil {\n   168→\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n   169→\t\t}\n   170→\n   171→\t\t// Extract the fields this controller previously applied\n   172→\t\tpodApply, err := corev1ac.ExtractPod(pod, \"my-controller\")\n   173→\t\tif err != nil {\n   174→\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n   175→\t\t}\n   176→\n   177→\t\t// Modify in place - only changing what we need\n   178→\t\tpodApply.WithLabels(map[string]string{\n   179→\t\t\t\"updated-at\": time.Now().Format(time.RFC3339),\n   180→\t\t})\n   181→\n   182→\t\t// Re-apply the modified configuration\n   183→\t\t_, err = r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Apply(\n   184→\t\t\tctx,\n   185→\t\t\tpodApply,\n   186→\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n   187→\t\t)\n   188→\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n   189→\t}\n   190→\n   191→The Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod, ExtractDeployment) read the managed\n   192→fields from an object and return an apply configuration containing only the\n   193→fields owned by the specified field manager. This allows controllers to:\n   194→  - Preserve fields they previously set\n   195→  - Make targeted modifications without affecting other fields\n   196→  - Avoid accidentally removing fields that were intentionally set\n   197→\n   198→Note that an extracted apply configuration may contain fewer fields than\n   199→originally applied if another field manager has since taken ownership of some\n   200→fields (via Force option).\n   201→\n   202→# Conflict Resolution\n   203→\n   204→When multiple field managers attempt to modify the same field, Server-side Apply\n   205→returns a conflict error. There are two ways to handle conflicts:\n   206→\n   207→1. Retry with Force: Set Force: true in ApplyOptions to take ownership of\n   208→conflicting fields. Use this when you want to override other managers:\n   209→\n   210→\t_, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n   211→\t\tctx,\n   212→\t\tpodApply,\n   213→\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{\n   214→\t\t\tFieldManager: \"my-controller\",\n   215→\t\t\tForce:        true,\n   216→\t\t},\n   217→\t)\n   218→\n   219→2. Remove the conflicting fields: If your controller doesn't need to own those\n   220→fields, simply don't set them in your apply configuration.\n   221→\n   222→# Package Structure\n   223→\n   224→The package is organized by API group and version:\n   225→  - applyconfigurations/core/v1 - Core API types (Pod, Service, ConfigMap, etc.)\n   226→  - applyconfigurations/apps/v1 - Apps API types (Deployment, StatefulSet, etc.)\n   227→  - applyconfigurations/batch/v1 - Batch API types (Job, CronJob)\n   228→  - applyconfigurations/meta/v1 - Metadata types (ObjectMeta, TypeMeta, etc.)\n   229→  - And similar subpackages for other API groups\n   230→\n   231→Each subpackage provides:\n   232→  - Apply configuration struct types (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration)\n   233→  - Constructor functions (e.g., Pod(name, namespace string))\n   234→  - Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod(pod *corev1.Pod, fieldManager string))\n   235→  - With builder methods for all fields\n   236→*/\n   237→package applyconfigurations\n   238→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Unlike traditional create/update operations that replace the entire\nresource, Server-side Apply performs a merge of the fields you specify with\nthe existing resource, tracked by field managers.\n\nThis package provides generated apply configuration types that are specifically\ndesigned to work with Server-side Apply. Each apply configuration type\ncorresponds to a Kubernetes API type and is named with an \"ApplyConfiguration\"\nsuffix (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration for Pod).\n\n# The Zero-Value Problem\n\nStandard Go structs cannot be used directly with Server-side Apply due to the\nzero-value problem. In Go, all struct fields have zero values when not\nexplicitly set. For example:\n\n\t// Using a standard Go struct\n\tpod := &corev1.Pod{\n\t\tSpec: corev1.PodSpec{\n\t\t\tActiveDeadlineSeconds: nil, // Is this intentionally unset, or just zero?\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\nWhen encoding this to JSON, there is no way to distinguish between:\n  - A field that was intentionally set to nil (meaning: \"I want to remove this field\")\n  - A field that was never set (meaning: \"I don't care about this field\")\n\nThis distinction is critical for Server-side Apply because it determines field\nownership. If a zero-valued field is included in an apply request, the field\nmanager takes ownership of it, potentially causing conflicts with other field\nmanagers.\n\n# Apply Configuration Types\n\nApply configuration types solve this problem by making all fields pointers or\nslices, allowing the distinction between \"not set\" (nil) and \"explicitly set\":\n\n\t// Using an apply configuration type\n\tpod := corev1.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n\t\tWithSpec(corev1.PodSpec().\n\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1.Container().\n\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n\nIn the example above, only the Name and Image fields of the Container are set.\nOther fields like Command, Args, and WorkingDir remain nil, indicating they\nshould not be included in the apply request.\n\n# With Functions\n\nEach apply configuration type provides builder methods prefixed with \"With\" for\nsetting fields. These methods:\n  - Accept the field value as a parameter\n  - Set the field to that value\n  - Return the receiver for method chaining\n\nFor example:\n\n\tcontainer := corev1.Container().\n\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\").\n\t\tWithPorts(\n\t\t\tcorev1.ContainerPort().\n\t\t\t\tWithContainerPort(80).\n\t\t\t\tWithProtocol(\"TCP\"),\n\t\t)\n\nThis pattern allows you to build complex configurations with a fluent API while\nensuring only explicitly set fields are included in the apply request.\n\n# Using Apply Configurations with Typed Clients\n\nThe typed clients in k8s.io/client-go/kubernetes/typed provide Apply methods\nthat accept apply configurations directly:\n\n\timport (\n\t\t\"context\"\n\t\tmetav1 \"k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/apis/meta/v1\"\n\t\tcorev1ac \"k8s.io/client-go/applyconfigurations/core/v1\"\n\t)\n\n\t// Create an apply configuration\n\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(\"my-pod\", \"default\").\n\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n\t\t\t\tWithName(\"nginx\").\n\t\t\t\tWithImage(\"nginx:1.21\")))\n\n\t// Apply to the cluster\n\tpod, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n\t\tcontext.TODO(),\n\t\tpodApply,\n\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t)\n\nThe FieldManager in ApplyOptions identifies the actor making changes. This is\nrequired for Server-side Apply to track field ownership. If another field\nmanager has already set a field you're trying to modify, you'll receive a\nconflict error unless you use the Force option.\n\n# Controller Support\n\nThis package supports two mechanisms for using Server-side Apply in controllers:\n\nMechanism 1: Recreate Apply Configuration Each Reconciliation\n\nIn this approach, the controller builds the complete apply configuration from\nscratch on each reconciliation:\n\n\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n\t\t// Fetch the source of truth (e.g., a custom resource)\n\t\tmyResource := &myv1.MyResource{}\n\t\tif err := r.Client.Get(ctx, req.NamespacedName, myResource); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Build apply configuration from scratch\n\t\tpodApply := corev1ac.Pod(myResource.Name, myResource.Namespace).\n\t\t\tWithSpec(corev1ac.PodSpec().\n\t\t\t\tWithContainers(corev1ac.Container().\n\t\t\t\t\tWithName(\"app\").\n\t\t\t\t\tWithImage(myResource.Spec.Image)))\n\n\t\t// Apply - fields not specified will not be affected\n\t\t_, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(myResource.Namespace).Apply(\n\t\t\tctx,\n\t\t\tpodApply,\n\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t}\n\nThis approach is simple and stateless. Each reconciliation declares the desired\nstate without needing to know the current state.\n\nMechanism 2: Extract/Modify-in-Place/Apply Workflow\n\nFor controllers that need to preserve existing field values while making\ntargeted modifications, the Extract functions provide a way to read back\npreviously applied fields:\n\n\tfunc (r *MyReconciler) Reconcile(ctx context.Context, req ctrl.Request) (ctrl.Result, error) {\n\t\t// Fetch the current pod\n\t\tpod, err := r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Get(ctx, req.Name, metav1.GetOptions{})\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, client.IgnoreNotFound(err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Extract the fields this controller previously applied\n\t\tpodApply, err := corev1ac.ExtractPod(pod, \"my-controller\")\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Modify in place - only changing what we need\n\t\tpodApply.WithLabels(map[string]string{\n\t\t\t\"updated-at\": time.Now().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t\t})\n\n\t\t// Re-apply the modified configuration\n\t\t_, err = r.Clientset.CoreV1().Pods(req.Namespace).Apply(\n\t\t\tctx,\n\t\t\tpodApply,\n\t\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{FieldManager: \"my-controller\"},\n\t\t)\n\t\treturn ctrl.Result{}, err\n\t}\n\nThe Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod, ExtractDeployment) read the managed\nfields from an object and return an apply configuration containing only the\nfields owned by the specified field manager. This allows controllers to:\n  - Preserve fields they previously set\n  - Make targeted modifications without affecting other fields\n  - Avoid accidentally removing fields that were intentionally set\n\nNote that an extracted apply configuration may contain fewer fields than\noriginally applied if another field manager has since taken ownership of some\nfields (via Force option).\n\n# Conflict Resolution\n\nWhen multiple field managers attempt to modify the same field, Server-side Apply\nreturns a conflict error. There are two ways to handle conflicts:\n\n1. Retry with Force: Set Force: true in ApplyOptions to take ownership of\nconflicting fields. Use this when you want to override other managers:\n\n\t_, err := clientset.CoreV1().Pods(\"default\").Apply(\n\t\tctx,\n\t\tpodApply,\n\t\tmetav1.ApplyOptions{\n\t\t\tFieldManager: \"my-controller\",\n\t\t\tForce:        true,\n\t\t},\n\t)\n\n2. Remove the conflicting fields: If your controller doesn't need to own those\nfields, simply don't set them in your apply configuration.\n\n# Package Structure\n\nThe package is organized by API group and version:\n  - applyconfigurations/core/v1 - Core API types (Pod, Service, ConfigMap, etc.)\n  - applyconfigurations/apps/v1 - Apps API types (Deployment, StatefulSet, etc.)\n  - applyconfigurations/batch/v1 - Batch API types (Job, CronJob)\n  - applyconfigurations/meta/v1 - Metadata types (ObjectMeta, TypeMeta, etc.)\n  - And similar subpackages for other API groups\n\nEach subpackage provides:\n  - Apply configuration struct types (e.g., PodApplyConfiguration)\n  - Constructor functions (e.g., Pod(name, namespace string))\n  - Extract functions (e.g., ExtractPod(pod *corev1.Pod, fieldManager string))\n  - With builder methods for all fields\n*/\npackage applyconfigurations\n","numLines":238,"startLine":1,"totalLines":238}}}
